Domain card test

Which card the agentic identifier attaches to each chatbot, and whether it is the right one. Made 2026-11-10 by test_cards.py.

How a card is chosen

1. Exact versionfull card (all 4 domains)
→ if version Unknown
2. Familyfields shared by the line's known models (needs 2+ known versions)
→ if family Unknown
3. Developerfields shared by the company's models
→ if nothing
4. No cardsent for review

A card is right when the chatbot's real model belongs to it (same version, family or developer), and most specific when no more specific card was possible with the models the identifier knew. Hidden models are never used for cards.

Key licence conditions
Conditions:
  • Users must be 18 or older and in an available region
  • Follow Google's Generative AI Prohibited Use Policy
Restrictions:
  • Free tier: Google uses prompts and responses to improve its products, and human reviewers may read them; do not submit sensitive, confidential or personal information
  • No developing models that compete with the Services, and no reverse engineering
  • Users in the EEA, Switzerland or UK must use paid services
Bank note: A bank must use the paid tier for anything involving customer data, because free-tier inputs can be read by Google reviewers. Prompts with no personal data (e.g. research prompts) are fine on the free tier.
Source: https://policies.google.com/terms/generative-ai/use-policy

Key licence conditions
Conditions:
  • When distributing Gemma or a Model Derivative, make the use restrictions (3.2) enforceable in your own terms and tell downstream users they apply (3.1)
  • Give all recipients a copy of the Terms; mark modified files (3.1)
  • Include a NOTICE file: 'Gemma is provided under and subject to the Gemma Terms of Use found at ai.google.dev/gemma/terms' (3.1; not required when offered only as a hosted service)

Restrictions:
  • Google may restrict usage it reasonably believes breaks the Terms, remotely or otherwise (3.2)
  • 'Model Derivatives' include models trained on Gemma's outputs or synthetic data to perform similarly to Gemma, e.g. distillation (1.1(e)). Outputs themselves are not derivatives
  • No rights to Google trademarks, and no implying Google endorsement (4.2)
  • Google can terminate for breach; you must then delete Gemma and any Model Derivatives (4.5)
  • Governed by California law, courts of Santa Clara County (4.6)

Compliance card

Developer of recordMeta Platforms, Inc. (Meta Platforms Ireland Ltd. for the EEA and Switzerland)

Key licence conditions
Conditions:
  • When distributing the model or a product using it, include a copy of the licence and prominently display 'Built with Llama' (1.b.i)
  • If outputs are used to create, train, fine-tune or improve an AI model that is distributed or made available, include 'Llama' at the beginning of that model's name (1.b.i)
  • Keep the notice: 'Llama 3.2 is licensed under the Llama 3.2 Community License, Copyright © Meta Platforms, Inc. All Rights Reserved.' (1.b.iii)
  • Follow applicable laws and the Llama 3.2 Acceptable Use Policy, which is part of the licence (1.b.iv)
Restrictions:
  • Organizations with over 700 million monthly active users (as of September 25, 2024) must request a separate licence from Meta (Section 2)
  • Multimodal Llama 3.2 models only: no licence for individuals living in, or companies based in, the European Union; end users of a product built with them are not affected (Acceptable Use Policy). llama3.2:3b is text-only, so this does not apply to it
  • All licences end if you sue Meta claiming Llama or its outputs infringe your IP (5.c)
  • Meta can terminate the licence for any breach; you must then delete the model (Section 6)
  • Governed by California law, California courts (Section 7)
Bank note: Same as Llama 3.1: allowed for a bank, but the Acceptable Use Policy bans unlicensed financial advice and discriminatory credit decisions, and customer letters must not be presented as written by a person.
Source: https://www.llama.com/llama3_2/use-policy
Base modelInstruction-tuned version of the Llama 3.2 3B pretrained model. The 3B was built by pruning and distillation, using Llama 3.1 8B and 70B outputs as training targets. Not a third-party fine-tune.

Key licence conditions
Conditions:
  • When distributing the model or a product using it, include a copy of the licence and show 'Built with Llama' (1.b.i)
  • If outputs are used to train or improve an AI model that is shared, that model's name must start with 'Llama' (1.b.i)
  • Keep the notice: 'Llama 3.1 is licensed under the Llama 3.1 Community License, Copyright © Meta Platforms, Inc. All Rights Reserved.' (1.b.iii)
  • Follow applicable laws and the Acceptable Use Policy, which is part of the licence (1.b.iv)
Restrictions:
  • Organizations with over 700 million monthly active users (as of July 23, 2024) must request a separate licence from Meta (Section 2)
  • No trademark rights except using 'Llama' as required above (5.a)
  • All licences end if you sue Meta claiming Llama or its outputs infringe your IP (5.c)
  • Meta can terminate the licence for any breach; you must then delete the model (Section 6)
  • Governed by California law, California courts (Section 7)
Bank note: Allowed for a bank (all Canadian banks are far below 700M users), but conditional: the Acceptable Use Policy bans unlicensed financial advice and discriminatory credit decisions, and customer letters must not be presented as written by a person. A vendor swapping in a Llama fine-tune brings these obligations with it.

Key licence conditions
Conditions:
  • Users must be 18 or older and in an available region
  • Follow Google's Generative AI Prohibited Use Policy
Restrictions:
  • Free tier: Google uses prompts and responses to improve its products, and human reviewers may read them; do not submit sensitive, confidential or personal information
  • No developing models that compete with the Services, and no reverse engineering
  • Users in the EEA, Switzerland or UK must use paid services
Bank note: A bank must use the paid tier for anything involving customer data, because free-tier inputs can be read by Google reviewers. Prompts with no personal data (e.g. research prompts) are fine on the free tier.
Gemini 3.1 Pro, Gemini 3.8 Flash
Source: https://policies.google.com/terms/generative-ai/use-policy
Conditions:
  • When distributing Gemma or a Model Derivative, make the use restrictions (3.2) enforceable in your own terms and tell downstream users they apply (3.1)
  • Give all recipients a copy of the Terms; mark modified files (3.1)
  • Include a NOTICE file: 'Gemma is provided under and subject to the Gemma Terms of Use found at ai.google.dev/gemma/terms' (3.1; not required when offered only as a hosted service)
  • Follow the Prohibited Use Policy and applicable law (3.2)
Restrictions:
  • Google may restrict usage it reasonably believes breaks the Terms, remotely or otherwise (3.2)
  • 'Model Derivatives' include models trained on Gemma's outputs or synthetic data to perform similarly to Gemma, e.g. distillation (1.1(e)). Outputs themselves are not derivatives
  • No rights to Google trademarks, and no implying Google endorsement (4.2)
  • Google can terminate for breach; you must then delete Gemma and any Model Derivatives (4.5)
  • Governed by California law, courts of Santa Clara County (4.6)
Bank note: Allowed for a bank, but stricter than Apache 2.0: Gemma cannot make automated decisions about credit or other finance matters affecting customers, and cannot give unlicensed financial advice. A vendor swapping in a Gemma-based model brings these restrictions with it.

Gemma 3 4B
Source: https://ai.google.dev/gemma/terms
Key licence conditions
Conditions:
  • Users must be 18 or older and in an available region
  • Follow Google's Generative AI Prohibited Use Policy
Restrictions:
  • Free tier: Google uses prompts and responses to improve its products, and human reviewers may read them; do not submit sensitive, confidential or personal information
  • No developing models that compete with the Services, and no reverse engineering
  • Users in the EEA, Switzerland or UK must use paid services
Bank note: A bank must use the paid tier for anything involving customer data, because free-tier inputs can be read by Google reviewers. Prompts with no personal data (e.g. research prompts) are fine on the free tier.
Gemini 3.8 Flash
Source: https://policies.google.com/terms/generative-ai/use-policy
Conditions:
  • When distributing Gemma or a Model Derivative, make the use restrictions (3.2) enforceable in your own terms and tell downstream users they apply (3.1)
  • Give all recipients a copy of the Terms; mark modified files (3.1)
  • Include a NOTICE file: 'Gemma is provided under and subject to the Gemma Terms of Use found at ai.google.dev/gemma/terms' (3.1; not required when offered only as a hosted service)
  • Follow the Prohibited Use Policy and applicable law (3.2)
Restrictions:
  • Google may restrict usage it reasonably believes breaks the Terms, remotely or otherwise (3.2)
  • 'Model Derivatives' include models trained on Gemma's outputs or synthetic data to perform similarly to Gemma, e.g. distillation (1.1(e)). Outputs themselves are not derivatives
  • No rights to Google trademarks, and no implying Google endorsement (4.2)
  • Google can terminate for breach; you must then delete Gemma and any Model Derivatives (4.5)
  • Governed by California law, courts of Santa Clara County (4.6)
Bank note: Allowed for a bank, but stricter than Apache 2.0: Gemma cannot make automated decisions about credit or other finance matters affecting customers, and cannot give unlicensed financial advice. A vendor swapping in a Gemma-based model brings these restrictions with it.

Key licence conditions
Conditions:
  • When distributing the model or a product using it, include a copy of the licence and prominently display 'Built with Llama' (1.b.i)
  • If outputs are used to create, train, fine-tune or improve an AI model that is distributed or made available, include 'Llama' at the beginning of that model's name (1.b.i)
  • Keep the notice: 'Llama 3.2 is licensed under the Llama 3.2 Community License, Copyright © Meta Platforms, Inc. All Rights Reserved.' (1.b.iii)
  • Follow applicable laws and the Llama 3.2 Acceptable Use Policy, which is part of the licence (1.b.iv)
Restrictions:
  • Organizations with over 700 million monthly active users (as of September 25, 2024) must request a separate licence from Meta (Section 2)
  • Multimodal Llama 3.2 models only: no licence for individuals living in, or companies based in, the European Union; end users of a product built with them are not affected (Acceptable Use Policy). llama3.2:3b is text-only, so this does not apply to it
  • All licences end if you sue Meta claiming Llama or its outputs infringe your IP (5.c)
  • Meta can terminate the licence for any breach; you must then delete the model (Section 6)
  • Governed by California law, California courts (Section 7)
Bank note: Same as Llama 3.1: allowed for a bank, but the Acceptable Use Policy bans unlicensed financial advice and discriminatory credit decisions, and customer letters must not be presented as written by a person.

From Llama 3.1 8B, the only known model in this group
Key licence conditions
Conditions:
  • When distributing the model or a product using it, include a copy of the licence and show 'Built with Llama' (1.b.i)
  • If outputs are used to train or improve an AI model that is shared, that model's name must start with 'Llama' (1.b.i)
  • Keep the notice: 'Llama 3.1 is licensed under the Llama 3.1 Community License, Copyright © Meta Platforms, Inc. All Rights Reserved.' (1.b.iii)
  • Follow applicable laws and the Acceptable Use Policy, which is part of the licence (1.b.iv)
Restrictions:
  • Organizations with over 700 million monthly active users (as of July 23, 2024) must request a separate licence from Meta (Section 2)
  • No trademark rights except using 'Llama' as required above (5.a)
  • All licences end if you sue Meta claiming Llama or its outputs infringe your IP (5.c)
  • Meta can terminate the licence for any breach; you must then delete the model (Section 6)
  • Governed by California law, California courts (Section 7)
Bank note: Allowed for a bank (all Canadian banks are far below 700M users), but conditional: the Acceptable Use Policy bans unlicensed financial advice and discriminatory credit decisions, and customer letters must not be presented as written by a person. A vendor swapping in a Llama fine-tune brings these obligations with it.
Source: https://github.com/meta-llama/llama-models/blob/main/models/llama3_1/USE_POLICY.md

From GPT-5.6 Luna, the only known model in this group
Key licence conditions
Conditions:
  • Follow the Usage Policies
  • Customer owns its inputs and outputs
Restrictions:
  • No tailored advice that requires a licence (e.g. legal, medical) without a licensed professional involved (Usage Policies)
  • No automating high-stakes decisions in financial activities, credit, insurance or employment without human review (Usage Policies)
  • No profiling or distributing people's private information without authorization (Usage Policies)
  • No using outputs to build models that compete with OpenAI; internal classifiers are an allowed exception (Services Agreement)
  • Provided 'as is' with no warranty; governed by California law, arbitration in San Francisco (Services Agreement)
Bank note: Usable by a bank through a paid account. Credit and other financial decisions need human review, and tailored financial advice needs a licensed professional involved. OpenAI also covers customers against third-party IP claims (with exclusions).
Source: https://openai.com/policies/usage-policies/
